# Research Group subset-b-009461

# sources/test-tools/syzkaller/pkg/ifuzz/powerpc/powerpc.go

## Purpose

`powerpc.go` implements the syzkaller ifuzz backend for PPC64/PowerISA instruction generation and decoding. It defines the instruction model used by generated PowerPC tables, registers the architecture with the shared `iset` package, emits randomized machine code, recognizes instruction byte streams, and provides helper macros for pseudo-instruction generators that need to materialize integer values in registers or memory.

## Important APIs, Types, and Functions

Core data types are `InsnBits`, `InsnField`, `Insn`, `insnSetMap`, and `InsnSet`. `Insn` carries name, privilege and pseudo flags, primary and suffix opcode/mask data, field metadata, a back-pointer to the instruction map, and an optional pseudo generator. `InsnSet` exposes `GetInsns`, `Decode`, and `DecodeExt` to satisfy the ifuzz architecture interface. Encoding APIs are `Insn.Encode`, `encodeOpcode`, `encodeBits`, `enc`, and `encOpcode`. Registration and metadata APIs are `Register`, `Info`, `mode`, and `uint32toBytes`. Instruction-map helpers `ld64`, `ld32`, `ldgpr32`, and `sc` build common PowerPC sequences used by pseudo instructions.

## Control Flow

`Register` is called by generated instruction-table packages. It builds a name-to-instruction map, stores that map back into every instruction, appends pseudo instructions through `initPseudo`, adds all instructions to `modeInsns`, and installs the set into `iset.Arches[iset.ArchPowerPC]`. `Decode` first requires at least four bytes, reads little-endian instruction words, detects PowerISA prefixed instructions via the six-bit prefix opcode, and then compares masks/opcodes against known instructions. Prefixed decode requires eight bytes and validates both prefix and suffix words; non-prefixed decode returns four bytes on the first opcode/mask match.

Encoding starts at `Insn.Encode`. Pseudo instructions delegate to their generator. Normal instructions encode the primary word and, for prefixed opcodes, a suffix word. `encodeOpcode` starts with fixed opcode bits, optionally randomizes unknown bits when memory regions exist, fills each declared field with random values, forces register-pair fields even, and may prepend register materialization sequences for memory-related register fields. `enc` and `encOpcode` provide deterministic field-value encoding for pseudo helpers.

## State and Persistence Behavior

The backend keeps runtime state in `InsnSet.Insns`, `InsnSet.modeInsns`, `InsnSet.insnMap`, and each instruction's `insnMap` pointer. There is no filesystem persistence. Generated byte slices are transient. The global integration state is `iset.Arches`, which receives the PowerPC backend during registration. Randomness comes from the caller-provided `rand.Rand`, while memory-aware immediate/register values come from `iset.Config` and `iset.GenerateInt`.

## Dependencies and Integration Points

The file depends on `encoding/binary`, `errors`, `fmt`, `math/rand`, and `github.com/google/syzkaller/pkg/ifuzz/iset`. It integrates with generated PowerISA instruction data, with `pseudo.go` through `initPseudo` and instruction-map helper methods, and with syzkaller's architecture-agnostic ifuzz scheduling through `iset.Arches`, `iset.ModeInsns`, `iset.Config`, and `iset.Insn`. The helper methods assume base instructions such as `addis`, `ori`, `rldicr`, `stw`, and `sc` exist in the generated map.

## Risks and Edge Cases

Decode performs a linear scan over the instruction list, so ordering matters when masks overlap. Prefixed decode validates the suffix against any prefixed instruction rather than the matched prefix instruction's own suffix metadata, which could admit mismatched prefix/suffix pairs if encodings overlap. `encOpcode` accepts a field parameter but loops over `insn.Fields`, so suffix field encoding may be wrong for prefixed suffixes. Unknown-bit randomization is gated on `MemRegions`, which makes encoding behavior differ between memory-aware and plain configurations. Several helper methods index the instruction map by string and will panic if generated tables omit expected mnemonics.

## Test Signals

Strong tests should register a small instruction set and verify `Decode` length/error behavior for short, valid, invalid, and prefixed byte streams. Encoding tests should assert little-endian output, fixed mask preservation, register-pair evenness, deterministic `enc` output, and correct `ld64`/`ld32` sequences. Integration tests should ensure `Register` adds pseudo instructions, populates `modeInsns`, and publishes `iset.Arches[iset.ArchPowerPC]`.
<!-- END_FILE_RESEARCH: sources/test-tools/syzkaller/pkg/ifuzz/powerpc/powerpc.go -->

<!-- BEGIN_FILE_RESEARCH: sources/test-tools/syzkaller/pkg/ifuzz/powerpc/pseudo.go -->
# sources/test-tools/syzkaller/pkg/ifuzz/powerpc/pseudo.go

## Purpose

`pseudo.go` adds privileged pseudo-instructions to the PowerPC ifuzz backend. These are not single ISA encodings; they synthesize short instruction streams that exercise hypercalls, syscalls, ultracalls, RTAS calls, and `rfid` return-from-interrupt behavior. The file complements `powerpc.go` by using the registered instruction map and deterministic field encoders to build realistic multi-instruction byte sequences.

## Important APIs, Types, and Functions

Constants define hypercall and special-purpose-register values: `MaxHcall`, `SprnSrr0`, and `SprnSrr1`. `InsnSet.initPseudo` appends `PSEUDO_hypercall`, `PSEUDO_syscall`, `PSEUDO_ultracall`, `PSEUDO_rtas`, and `PSEUDO_rfid` as privileged pseudo instructions. The local `generator` type carries the instruction map, ifuzz config, random source, and accumulated text. Helper methods are `makeGen`, `byte`, `sc`, `rtas`, and `rfid`.

## Control Flow

`Register` in `powerpc.go` calls `initPseudo` after building the instruction map. Each pseudo instruction stores a closure that constructs a `generator`, emits a sequence, and returns `gen.text`. `sc` chooses a hypercall range, loads the call number into GPR3, loads randomized arguments into later GPRs, and emits the `sc` instruction with the requested level. `rtas` writes a token and randomized words to generated memory through `ldgpr32`, loads the custom `H_RTAS` hypercall number and argument address, then emits a level-1 system call. `rfid` loads randomized SRR0 and SRR1 values, writes them with `mtspr`, and finally emits `rfid`.

## State and Persistence Behavior

Pseudo generation is transient. Each call creates a fresh `generator` with a mutable `text` byte slice. It reads from `iset.Config` for memory-region-aware integer generation and from `rand.Rand` for register, token, hypercall, and argument selection. Persistent registration state is the appended pseudo `Insn` records in `InsnSet.Insns`; their `Priv` and `Pseudo` flags affect instruction selection and metadata but no data is written to disk.

## Dependencies and Integration Points

The file depends on `math/rand` and `github.com/google/syzkaller/pkg/ifuzz/iset`. It relies heavily on helper methods from `powerpc.go`: `ld64`, `ld32`, `ldgpr32`, `sc`, and deterministic `Insn.enc`. It also assumes generated instruction names `mtspr`, `rfid`, and the load/store/arithmetic mnemonics used by the helpers are present in `insnSetMap`. The resulting pseudo instructions integrate with the shared ifuzz mode/type indexing through `modeInsns.Add` after `initPseudo` returns.

## Risks and Edge Cases

The map lookups are unchecked, so missing generated mnemonics cause panics during pseudo generation. `rtas` derives `reg+1` without bounding against the 31-register limit, so high random registers can address an invalid paired register number in the generated field value. Hypercall range selection intentionally includes sparse ranges and may generate call numbers that are architecturally valid but unsupported by a given target. `rfid` writes arbitrary SRR0/SRR1 values, which is useful for fuzzing but can quickly leave normal control flow. All pseudo instructions are privileged and should be filtered when a non-privileged mode is desired.

## Test Signals

Tests should confirm `initPseudo` appends exactly the expected pseudo names, marks them `Priv` and `Pseudo`, and produces non-empty byte streams. Focused tests can seed `rand.Rand` to make `sc`, `rtas`, and `rfid` deterministic enough to decode the emitted instructions. Integration signals include successful generation when all required base mnemonics are present and graceful detection in tests when a reduced instruction map omits one of those dependencies.
<!-- END_FILE_RESEARCH: sources/test-tools/syzkaller/pkg/ifuzz/powerpc/pseudo.go -->

<!-- BEGIN_FILE_RESEARCH: sources/test-tools/syzkaller/pkg/ifuzz/riscv64/gen/gen.go -->
# sources/test-tools/syzkaller/pkg/ifuzz/riscv64/gen/gen.go

## Purpose

`gen.go` is the command-line generator that converts riscv-unified-db instruction YAML into syzkaller's `pkg/ifuzz/riscv64` generated instruction table. It walks a source `inst` tree, accepts instruction descriptors with 32-bit match patterns, translates fixed bits and variable bit ranges into `riscv64.Insn` values, serializes the resulting slice as Go code, and writes it atomically to the requested output path.

## Important APIs, Types, and Functions

`instYAML` is the narrow YAML projection used by this tool. It captures `kind`, `name`, `encoding.match`, `encoding.variables[].name`, `encoding.variables[].location`, and `access.u`/`access.vu`. `main` handles argument validation, directory walking, YAML reads, unmarshalling, filtering, output rendering, serialization, and atomic file write. `buildInsn` builds one `riscv64.Insn`. `parseLocations` expands single or split bit locations into `InsnField` entries. `parseRange` parses `hi-lo` location strings and returns the field start and length.

## Control Flow

The command requires exactly two arguments: the riscv-unified-db `spec/std/isa/inst` directory and an output Go file. `filepath.WalkDir` skips directories and non-YAML files. For each YAML file, it reads and unmarshals metadata; IO or YAML errors for individual files are ignored by returning nil from the walker. Non-`instruction` entries and match strings not exactly 32 characters long are skipped. `buildInsn` scans the 32-character match string from MSB to LSB, constructing opcode and mask bits for `0` and `1`, accepting `-` as variable, and rejecting any other character. Variables are parsed into fields, privilege is computed from U/VU access, and accepted instructions are appended.

After walking, `main` writes a generated Go preamble, imports the `riscv64` package dot-style, emits an `init` function that registers `insns_riscv64`, serializes the instruction slice with `serializer.Write`, and persists the result through `osutil.WriteFileAtomically`. It reports the generated instruction count on stderr.

## State and Persistence Behavior

State is local to the generator process: the `insns` slice, each decoded `instYAML`, and the output buffer. The only persistent side effect is replacing the output Go file atomically. The generated file becomes runtime registration state when compiled, because its `init` calls `Register`. The tool does not cache source scans and does not persist diagnostics for skipped or malformed YAML entries.

## Dependencies and Integration Points

The generator depends on the standard `bytes`, `fmt`, `io/fs`, `os`, `path/filepath`, and `strings` packages; `gopkg.in/yaml.v3`; and syzkaller packages `pkg/ifuzz/riscv64`, `pkg/osutil`, `pkg/serializer`, and `pkg/tool`. It integrates the riscv-unified-db schema with syzkaller's RISC-V ifuzz instruction model. Its output is expected to live in a generated package that registers with the runtime RISC-V instruction set.

## Risks and Edge Cases

Several failures are intentionally silent: unreadable YAML, unmarshal errors, non-instruction entries, bad match lengths, unsupported match characters, and bad variable locations are all skipped without per-file diagnostics. The parser only supports `hi-lo` ranges and `|`-separated range fragments; single-bit locations such as `5` are rejected. The `start` field is set to `hi`, matching the existing `riscv64.InsnField` convention, but any mismatch in downstream bit numbering would corrupt generated operands. The generator ignores YAML constraints like `left_shift`, `not`, reserved values, xlen requirements, and most extension metadata, so the generated fuzzer table is an encoding-oriented approximation rather than a full ISA validator.

## Test Signals

Useful tests should feed a temporary YAML tree containing valid 32-bit instructions, malformed match strings, split locations, non-instruction entries, unreadable or bad YAML files, and 16-bit compressed descriptors. Expected signals are generated instruction count, opcode/mask values, field names and ranges, privilege classification from access modes, and atomic output content containing `Register(insns_riscv64)`. Unit tests for `parseRange` and `parseLocations` should cover reversed ranges, missing hyphens, invalid numbers, and split field naming.
<!-- END_FILE_RESEARCH: sources/test-tools/syzkaller/pkg/ifuzz/riscv64/gen/gen.go -->

<!-- BEGIN_FILE_RESEARCH: sources/test-tools/syzkaller/pkg/ifuzz/riscv64/gen/inst/B/andn.yaml -->
# sources/test-tools/syzkaller/pkg/ifuzz/riscv64/gen/inst/B/andn.yaml

## Purpose

`andn.yaml` is a riscv-unified-db YAML descriptor for the `andn` instruction. It is a `instruction` entry in the `B` instruction subset and represents a Boolean bit-manipulation operation. Its long name is "AND with inverted operand". The description says: Performs the bitwise logical AND operation between `xs1` and the bitwise inversion of `xs2`.

## Important APIs, Types, and Functions

The file is data consumed by `pkg/ifuzz/riscv64/gen/gen.go`, not executable code. The generator unmarshals it into `instYAML`, reading `kind`, `name`, `encoding.match`, `encoding.variables`, and `access.u`/`access.vu`. For this descriptor, `definedBy` resolves to `Zbb, Zbkb`, the assembly form is `xd, xs1, xs2`, and the encoded variable fields are: No variable fields are declared; all encoding bits are fixed by the match pattern.

## Control Flow

During generation, `filepath.WalkDir` finds this YAML file, `yaml.Unmarshal` populates the instruction metadata, and `buildInsn` converts the match string into an opcode and mask. The source does not expose an `encoding.match` pattern, so the generator would skip it. Each declared variable range is passed through `parseLocations`; split locations produce multiple `InsnField` entries with suffixed names so the fuzzer can fill discontiguous bitfields. If the match length is not 32 bits, the current generator skips the file, which is relevant for compressed 16-bit descriptors.

## State and Persistence Behavior

This source has no runtime state or persistence of its own. Its persistent effect is indirect: when accepted by the generator, it contributes one `riscv64.Insn` to the generated Go table, with `Name=andn`, fixed opcode/mask bits from the match pattern, generated fields from the variable list, `AsUInt32` initialized to the opcode, and `Priv` derived from access mode. The YAML includes semantic text but the current generator persists only encoding, name, fields, and privilege state.

## Dependencies and Integration Points

The descriptor depends on the riscv-unified-db schema (`inst_schema.json`) and is integrated through `gopkg.in/yaml.v3` unmarshalling in `gen.go`. The generated table imports `github.com/google/syzkaller/pkg/ifuzz/riscv64` and registers instructions with syzkaller's RISC-V ifuzz backend. Architectural integration is through `Zbb, Zbkb` extension availability, privilege access `s=always, u=always, vs=always, vu=always. The generator treats this as non-privileged because U/VU access is not `never`.`, and any operation/Sail snippets kept in the source for humans or future tooling.

## Risks and Edge Cases

The main risk is schema drift: fields such as `left_shift`, `not`, richer `definedBy` constraints, and semantic snippets are not modeled by `instYAML`, so they do not affect fuzz generation. A malformed or non-32-bit match pattern silently drops the instruction. Discontiguous immediate fields rely on range ordering from the YAML and simple `hi-lo` parsing, so encodings with single-bit locations or nonstandard location syntax may be skipped unless the generator is extended. Privilege classification is coarse because only U/VU `never` is checked. For this file, match length is 0, fixed bits are 0, variable bits are 0, and privileged classification is False.

## Test Signals

Good signals are generator tests that include this file, assert that `andn` appears or is intentionally skipped, and compare the generated opcode/mask/field list against the YAML. Decode/encode round trips in `pkg/ifuzz/riscv64` should validate that randomized operands preserve fixed bits. `operation()` is present and starts with `if (implemented?(ExtensionName::B) && (CSR[misa].B == 1'b0)) { raise (ExceptionCode::IllegalInstruct`. A Sail semantic snippet is also embedded for architecture-model traceability. Privilege tests should confirm that access modes map to the expected `Insn.Priv` value and that compressed or reserved encodings are handled intentionally.
<!-- END_FILE_RESEARCH: sources/test-tools/syzkaller/pkg/ifuzz/riscv64/gen/inst/B/andn.yaml -->

If the match length is not 32 bits, the current generator skips the file, which is relevant for compressed 16-bit descriptors.

## State and Persistence Behavior

This source has no runtime state or persistence of its own. Its persistent effect is indirect: when accepted by the generator, it contributes one `riscv64.Insn` to the generated Go table, with `Name=clmul`, fixed opcode/mask bits from the match pattern, generated fields from the variable list, `AsUInt32` initialized to the opcode, and `Priv` derived from access mode. The YAML includes semantic text but the current generator persists only encoding, name, fields, and privilege state.

## Dependencies and Integration Points

The descriptor depends on the riscv-unified-db schema (`inst_schema.json`) and is integrated through `gopkg.in/yaml.v3` unmarshalling in `gen.go`. The generated table imports `github.com/google/syzkaller/pkg/ifuzz/riscv64` and registers instructions with syzkaller's RISC-V ifuzz backend. Architectural integration is through `Zbc, Zbkc` extension availability, privilege access `s=always, u=always, vs=always, vu=always. The generator treats this as non-privileged because U/VU access is not `never`.`, and any operation/Sail snippets kept in the source for humans or future tooling.

The main risk is schema drift: fields such as `left_shift`, `not`, richer `definedBy` constraints, and semantic snippets are not modeled by `instYAML`, so they do not affect fuzz generation. Privilege classification is coarse because only U/VU `never` is checked. For this file, match length is 0, fixed bits are 0, variable bits are 0, and privileged classification is False.

## Test Signals

Good signals are generator tests that include this file, assert that `clmul` appears or is intentionally skipped, and compare the generated opcode/mask/field list against the YAML. Decode/encode round trips in `pkg/ifuzz/riscv64` should validate that randomized operands preserve fixed bits. `operation()` is present and starts with `if (implemented?(ExtensionName::B) && (CSR[misa].B == 1'b0)) { raise (ExceptionCode::IllegalInstruct`. A Sail semantic snippet is also embedded for architecture-model traceability. Privilege tests should confirm that access modes map to the expected `Insn.Priv` value and that compressed or reserved encodings are handled intentionally.

# sources/test-tools/syzkaller/pkg/ifuzz/riscv64/gen/inst/B/rev8.yaml

## Purpose

`rev8.yaml` is a riscv-unified-db YAML descriptor for the `rev8` instruction. It is a `instruction` entry in the `B` instruction subset and represents a byte-order reversal bit-manipulation operation. Its long name is "Byte-reverse register (RV64 encoding)". The description says: Reverses the order of the bytes in rs1. [NOTE] The rev8 mnemonic corresponds to different instruction encodings in RV32 and RV64. [NOTE] The byte-reverse operation is only available for the full register width. To emulate word-sized and halfword-sized byte-reversal, perform a `rev8 xd,xs1` followed by a `srai xd,xd,...

## Important APIs, Types, and Functions

The file is data consumed by `pkg/ifuzz/riscv64/gen/gen.go`, not executable code. The generator unmarshals it into `instYAML`, reading `kind`, `name`, `encoding.match`, `encoding.variables`, and `access.u`/`access.vu`. For this descriptor, `definedBy` resolves to `Zbb, Zbkb`, the assembly form is `xd, xs1`, and the encoded variable fields are: No variable fields are declared; all encoding bits are fixed by the match pattern.

## Control Flow

During generation, `filepath.WalkDir` finds this YAML file, `yaml.Unmarshal` populates the instruction metadata, and `buildInsn` converts the match string into an opcode and mask. The source does not expose an `encoding.match` pattern, so the generator would skip it. Each declared variable range is passed through `parseLocations`; split locations produce multiple `InsnField` entries with suffixed names so the fuzzer can fill discontiguous bitfields. If the match length is not 32 bits, the current generator skips the file, which is relevant for compressed 16-bit descriptors.

## State and Persistence Behavior

This source has no runtime state or persistence of its own. Its persistent effect is indirect: when accepted by the generator, it contributes one `riscv64.Insn` to the generated Go table, with `Name=rev8`, fixed opcode/mask bits from the match pattern, generated fields from the variable list, `AsUInt32` initialized to the opcode, and `Priv` derived from access mode. The YAML includes semantic text but the current generator persists only encoding, name, fields, and privilege state.

## Dependencies and Integration Points

The descriptor depends on the riscv-unified-db schema (`inst_schema.json`) and is integrated through `gopkg.in/yaml.v3` unmarshalling in `gen.go`. The generated table imports `github.com/google/syzkaller/pkg/ifuzz/riscv64` and registers instructions with syzkaller's RISC-V ifuzz backend. Architectural integration is through `Zbb, Zbkb` extension availability, privilege access `s=always, u=always, vs=always, vu=always. The generator treats this as non-privileged because U/VU access is not `never`.`, and any operation/Sail snippets kept in the source for humans or future tooling.

## Risks and Edge Cases

The main risk is schema drift: fields such as `left_shift`, `not`, richer `definedBy` constraints, and semantic snippets are not modeled by `instYAML`, so they do not affect fuzz generation. A malformed or non-32-bit match pattern silently drops the instruction. Discontiguous immediate fields rely on range ordering from the YAML and simple `hi-lo` parsing, so encodings with single-bit locations or nonstandard location syntax may be skipped unless the generator is extended. Privilege classification is coarse because only U/VU `never` is checked. For this file, match length is 0, fixed bits are 0, variable bits are 0, and privileged classification is False.

## Test Signals

Good signals are generator tests that include this file, assert that `rev8` appears or is intentionally skipped, and compare the generated opcode/mask/field list against the YAML. Decode/encode round trips in `pkg/ifuzz/riscv64` should validate that randomized operands preserve fixed bits. `operation()` is present and starts with `if (implemented?(ExtensionName::B) && (CSR[misa].B == 1'b0)) { raise (ExceptionCode::IllegalInstruct`. A Sail semantic snippet is also embedded for architecture-model traceability. Privilege tests should confirm that access modes map to the expected `Insn.Priv` value and that compressed or reserved encodings are handled intentionally.
<!-- END_FILE_RESEARCH: sources/test-tools/syzkaller/pkg/ifuzz/riscv64/gen/inst/B/rev8.yaml -->

# sources/test-tools/syzkaller/pkg/ifuzz/riscv64/gen/inst/B/xnor.yaml

## Purpose

`xnor.yaml` is a riscv-unified-db YAML descriptor for the `xnor` instruction. It is a `instruction` entry in the `B` instruction subset and represents a Boolean bit-manipulation operation. Its long name is "Exclusive NOR". The description says: Performs the bit-wise exclusive-NOR operation on xs1 and xs2.

## Important APIs, Types, and Functions

The file is data consumed by `pkg/ifuzz/riscv64/gen/gen.go`, not executable code. The generator unmarshals it into `instYAML`, reading `kind`, `name`, `encoding.match`, `encoding.variables`, and `access.u`/`access.vu`. For this descriptor, `definedBy` resolves to `Zbb, Zbkb`, the assembly form is `xd, xs1, xs2`, and the encoded variable fields are: No variable fields are declared; all encoding bits are fixed by the match pattern.

## Control Flow

During generation, `filepath.WalkDir` finds this YAML file, `yaml.Unmarshal` populates the instruction metadata, and `buildInsn` converts the match string into an opcode and mask. The source does not expose an `encoding.match` pattern, so the generator would skip it. Each declared variable range is passed through `parseLocations`; split locations produce multiple `InsnField` entries with suffixed names so the fuzzer can fill discontiguous bitfields. If the match length is not 32 bits, the current generator skips the file, which is relevant for compressed 16-bit descriptors.

## State and Persistence Behavior

This source has no runtime state or persistence of its own. Its persistent effect is indirect: when accepted by the generator, it contributes one `riscv64.Insn` to the generated Go table, with `Name=xnor`, fixed opcode/mask bits from the match pattern, generated fields from the variable list, `AsUInt32` initialized to the opcode, and `Priv` derived from access mode. The generated table imports `github.com/google/syzkaller/pkg/ifuzz/riscv64` and registers instructions with syzkaller's RISC-V ifuzz backend. Architectural integration is through `Zbb, Zbkb` extension availability, privilege access `s=always, u=always, vs=always, vu=always. The generator treats this as non-privileged because U/VU access is not `never`.`, and any operation/Sail snippets kept in the source for humans or future tooling.

The main risk is schema drift: fields such as `left_shift`, `not`, richer `definedBy` constraints, and semantic snippets are not modeled by `instYAML`, so they do not affect fuzz generation. ## Purpose

`c.jal.yaml` is a riscv-unified-db YAML descriptor for the `c.jal` instruction. It is a `instruction` entry in the `C` instruction subset and represents a compressed jump/control-transfer operation. Its long name is "Jump and Link". The description says: C.JAL is an RV32C-only instruction that performs the same operation as C.J, but additionally writes the address of the instruction following the jump (pc+2) to the link register, x1. ## Important APIs, Types, and Functions

The file is data consumed by `pkg/ifuzz/riscv64/gen/gen.go`, not executable code. The generator unmarshals it into `instYAML`, reading `kind`, `name`, `encoding.match`, `encoding.variables`, and `access.u`/`access.vu`. For this descriptor, `definedBy` resolves to `Zca`, the assembly form is `imm`, and the encoded variable fields are: `imm` at `12|8|10-9|6|7|2|11|5-3` (left_shift=1).

## Control Flow

During generation, `filepath.WalkDir` finds this YAML file, `yaml.Unmarshal` populates the instruction metadata, and `buildInsn` converts the match string into an opcode and mask. `encoding.match` is 16 bits with 5 fixed bits and 11 variable bits. Each declared variable range is passed through `parseLocations`; split locations produce multiple `InsnField` entries with suffixed names so the fuzzer can fill discontiguous bitfields. If the match length is not 32 bits, the current generator skips the file, which is relevant for compressed 16-bit descriptors.

## State and Persistence Behavior

This source has no runtime state or persistence of its own. Its persistent effect is indirect: when accepted by the generator, it contributes one `riscv64.Insn` to the generated Go table, with `Name=c.jal`, fixed opcode/mask bits from the match pattern, generated fields from the variable list, `AsUInt32` initialized to the opcode, and `Priv` derived from access mode. The generated table imports `github.com/google/syzkaller/pkg/ifuzz/riscv64` and registers instructions with syzkaller's RISC-V ifuzz backend. The generator treats this as non-privileged because U/VU access is not `never`.`, and any operation/Sail snippets kept in the source for humans or future tooling.

## Risks and Edge Cases

The main risk is schema drift: fields such as `left_shift`, `not`, richer `definedBy` constraints, and semantic snippets are not modeled by `instYAML`, so they do not affect fuzz generation. A malformed or non-32-bit match pattern silently drops the instruction. # sources/test-tools/syzkaller/pkg/ifuzz/riscv64/gen/inst/C/c.lw.yaml

## Purpose

`c.lw.yaml` is a riscv-unified-db YAML descriptor for the `c.lw` instruction. It is a `instruction` entry in the `C` instruction subset and represents a compressed memory access operation. Its long name is "Load word". The description says: Loads a 32-bit value from memory into register xd. It computes an effective address by adding the zero-extended offset, scaled by 4, to the base address in register xs1. It expands to `lw` `xd, offset(xs1)`.

## Important APIs, Types, and Functions

The file is data consumed by `pkg/ifuzz/riscv64/gen/gen.go`, not executable code. The generator unmarshals it into `instYAML`, reading `kind`, `name`, `encoding.match`, `encoding.variables`, and `access.u`/`access.vu`. For this descriptor, `definedBy` resolves to `Zca`, the assembly form is `xd, imm(xs1)`, and the encoded variable fields are: `imm` at `5|12-10|6` (left_shift=2); `xd` at `4-2`; `xs1` at `9-7`.

## Control Flow

During generation, `filepath.WalkDir` finds this YAML file, `yaml.Unmarshal` populates the instruction metadata, and `buildInsn` converts the match string into an opcode and mask. `encoding.match` is 16 bits with 5 fixed bits and 11 variable bits. Each declared variable range is passed through `parseLocations`; split locations produce multiple `InsnField` entries with suffixed names so the fuzzer can fill discontiguous bitfields. If the match length is not 32 bits, the current generator skips the file, which is relevant for compressed 16-bit descriptors.

## State and Persistence Behavior

This source has no runtime state or persistence of its own. Its persistent effect is indirect: when accepted by the generator, it contributes one `riscv64.Insn` to the generated Go table, with `Name=c.lw`, fixed opcode/mask bits from the match pattern, generated fields from the variable list, `AsUInt32` initialized to the opcode, and `Priv` derived from access mode. The YAML includes semantic text but the current generator persists only encoding, name, fields, and privilege state.

## Dependencies and Integration Points

The descriptor depends on the riscv-unified-db schema (`inst_schema.json`) and is integrated through `gopkg.in/yaml.v3` unmarshalling in `gen.go`. The generated table imports `github.com/google/syzkaller/pkg/ifuzz/riscv64` and registers instructions with syzkaller's RISC-V ifuzz backend. Architectural integration is through `Zca` extension availability, privilege access `s=always, u=always, vs=always, vu=always. The generator treats this as non-privileged because U/VU access is not `never`.`, and any operation/Sail snippets kept in the source for humans or future tooling.

## Risks and Edge Cases

The main risk is schema drift: fields such as `left_shift`, `not`, richer `definedBy` constraints, and semantic snippets are not modeled by `instYAML`, so they do not affect fuzz generation. A malformed or non-32-bit match pattern silently drops the instruction. Discontiguous immediate fields rely on range ordering from the YAML and simple `hi-lo` parsing, so encodings with single-bit locations or nonstandard location syntax may be skipped unless the generator is extended. Privilege classification is coarse because only U/VU `never` is checked. For this file, match length is 16, fixed bits are 5, variable bits are 11, and privileged classification is False.

## Test Signals

Good signals are generator tests that include this file, assert that `c.lw` appears or is intentionally skipped, and compare the generated opcode/mask/field list against the YAML. Decode/encode round trips in `pkg/ifuzz/riscv64` should validate that randomized operands preserve fixed bits. `operation()` is present and starts with `if (implemented?(ExtensionName::C) && (CSR[misa].C == 1'b0)) { raise(ExceptionCode::IllegalInstructi`. A Sail semantic snippet is also embedded for architecture-model traceability. Privilege tests should confirm that access modes map to the expected `Insn.Priv` value and that compressed or reserved encodings are handled intentionally.
<!-- END_FILE_RESEARCH: sources/test-tools/syzkaller/pkg/ifuzz/riscv64/gen/inst/C/c.lw.yaml -->

# sources/test-tools/syzkaller/pkg/ifuzz/riscv64/gen/inst/C/c.slli.yaml

## Purpose

`c.slli.yaml` is a riscv-unified-db YAML descriptor for the `c.slli` instruction. It is a `instruction` entry in the `C` instruction subset and represents a compressed arithmetic or register operation. Its long name is "Shift left logical immediate". The description says: Shift the value in xd left by shamt, and store the result back in xd. C.SLLI expands into `slli xd, xd, shamt`.

## Important APIs, Types, and Functions

The file is data consumed by `pkg/ifuzz/riscv64/gen/gen.go`, not executable code. The generator unmarshals it into `instYAML`, reading `kind`, `name`, `encoding.match`, `encoding.variables`, and `access.u`/`access.vu`. For this descriptor, `definedBy` resolves to `Zca`, the assembly form is `xd, shamt`, and the encoded variable fields are: No variable fields are declared; all encoding bits are fixed by the match pattern.

## Control Flow

During generation, `filepath.WalkDir` finds this YAML file, `yaml.Unmarshal` populates the instruction metadata, and `buildInsn` converts the match string into an opcode and mask. The source does not expose an `encoding.match` pattern, so the generator would skip it. Each declared variable range is passed through `parseLocations`; split locations produce multiple `InsnField` entries with suffixed names so the fuzzer can fill discontiguous bitfields. If the match length is not 32 bits, the current generator skips the file, which is relevant for compressed 16-bit descriptors.

## State and Persistence Behavior

This source has no runtime state or persistence of its own. Its persistent effect is indirect: when accepted by the generator, it contributes one `riscv64.Insn` to the generated Go table, with `Name=c.slli`, fixed opcode/mask bits from the match pattern, generated fields from the variable list, `AsUInt32` initialized to the opcode, and `Priv` derived from access mode. The YAML includes semantic text but the current generator persists only encoding, name, fields, and privilege state.

## Dependencies and Integration Points

The descriptor depends on the riscv-unified-db schema (`inst_schema.json`) and is integrated through `gopkg.in/yaml.v3` unmarshalling in `gen.go`. The generated table imports `github.com/google/syzkaller/pkg/ifuzz/riscv64` and registers instructions with syzkaller's RISC-V ifuzz backend. Architectural integration is through `Zca` extension availability, privilege access `s=always, u=always, vs=always, vu=always. The generator treats this as non-privileged because U/VU access is not `never`.`, and any operation/Sail snippets kept in the source for humans or future tooling.

## Risks and Edge Cases

The main risk is schema drift: fields such as `left_shift`, `not`, richer `definedBy` constraints, and semantic snippets are not modeled by `instYAML`, so they do not affect fuzz generation. A malformed or non-32-bit match pattern silently drops the instruction. Discontiguous immediate fields rely on range ordering from the YAML and simple `hi-lo` parsing, so encodings with single-bit locations or nonstandard location syntax may be skipped unless the generator is extended. Privilege classification is coarse because only U/VU `never` is checked. For this file, match length is 0, fixed bits are 0, variable bits are 0, and privileged classification is False.

## Test Signals

Good signals are generator tests that include this file, assert that `c.slli` appears or is intentionally skipped, and compare the generated opcode/mask/field list against the YAML. Decode/encode round trips in `pkg/ifuzz/riscv64` should validate that randomized operands preserve fixed bits. `operation()` is present and starts with `# shamt is between 0-63 X[xd] = X[xd] << shamt;`. A Sail semantic snippet is also embedded for architecture-model traceability. Privilege tests should confirm that access modes map to the expected `Insn.Priv` value and that compressed or reserved encodings are handled intentionally.
<!-- END_FILE_RESEARCH: sources/test-tools/syzkaller/pkg/ifuzz/riscv64/gen/inst/C/c.slli.yaml -->

# sources/test-tools/syzkaller/pkg/ifuzz/riscv64/gen/inst/D/fcvtmod.w.d.yaml

## Purpose

`fcvtmod.w.d.yaml` is a riscv-unified-db YAML descriptor for the `fcvtmod.w.d` instruction. It is a `instruction` entry in the `D` instruction subset and represents a double-precision floating-point conversion. Its long name is "Floating-Point Convert Double-Precision to Word with Modulo". The description says: The `fcvtmod.w.d` instruction always rounds towards zero. Bits 31:0 are taken from the rounded, unbounded two's complement result, then sign-extended to XLEN bits and written to integer register `xd`. ±∞ and NaN are converted to zero. Floating-point exception flags are raised the same as they would be for `fcvt.w.d`...

## Important APIs, Types, and Functions

Its long name is "Floating-Point Less Than Double-Precision". The description says: The `flt.d` instruction writes 1 to `xd` if `fs1` is less than `fs2`, and 0 otherwise. It is defined analogously to its single-precision counterpart, but operates on double-precision operands.

## Important APIs, Types, and Functions

The file is data consumed by `pkg/ifuzz/riscv64/gen/gen.go`, not executable code. The generator unmarshals it into `instYAML`, reading `kind`, `name`, `encoding.match`, `encoding.variables`, and `access.u`/`access.vu`. For this descriptor, `definedBy` resolves to `D, Zdinx`, the assembly form is `xd, fs1, fs2`, and the encoded variable fields are: `fs2` at `24-20`; `fs1` at `19-15`; `xd` at `11-7`.

## Control Flow

During generation, `filepath.WalkDir` finds this YAML file, `yaml.Unmarshal` populates the instruction metadata, and `buildInsn` converts the match string into an opcode and mask. `encoding.match` is 32 bits with 17 fixed bits and 15 variable bits. Each declared variable range is passed through `parseLocations`; split locations produce multiple `InsnField` entries with suffixed names so the fuzzer can fill discontiguous bitfields. If the match length is not 32 bits, the current generator skips the file, which is relevant for compressed 16-bit descriptors.

## State and Persistence Behavior

This source has no runtime state or persistence of its own. Its persistent effect is indirect: when accepted by the generator, it contributes one `riscv64.Insn` to the generated Go table, with `Name=flt.d`, fixed opcode/mask bits from the match pattern, generated fields from the variable list, `AsUInt32` initialized to the opcode, and `Priv` derived from access mode. The YAML includes semantic text but the current generator persists only encoding, name, fields, and privilege state.

## Dependencies and Integration Points

The descriptor depends on the riscv-unified-db schema (`inst_schema.json`) and is integrated through `gopkg.in/yaml.v3` unmarshalling in `gen.go`. The generated table imports `github.com/google/syzkaller/pkg/ifuzz/riscv64` and registers instructions with syzkaller's RISC-V ifuzz backend. Architectural integration is through `D, Zdinx` extension availability, privilege access `s=always, u=always, vs=always, vu=always. The generator treats this as non-privileged because U/VU access is not `never`.`, and any operation/Sail snippets kept in the source for humans or future tooling.

## Risks and Edge Cases

The main risk is schema drift: fields such as `left_shift`, `not`, richer `definedBy` constraints, and semantic snippets are not modeled by `instYAML`, so they do not affect fuzz generation. A malformed or non-32-bit match pattern silently drops the instruction. Discontiguous immediate fields rely on range ordering from the YAML and simple `hi-lo` parsing, so encodings with single-bit locations or nonstandard location syntax may be skipped unless the generator is extended. Privilege classification is coarse because only U/VU `never` is checked. For this file, match length is 32, fixed bits are 17, variable bits are 15, and privileged classification is False.

## Test Signals

Good signals are generator tests that include this file, assert that `flt.d` appears or is intentionally skipped, and compare the generated opcode/mask/field list against the YAML. Decode/encode round trips in `pkg/ifuzz/riscv64` should validate that randomized operands preserve fixed bits. `operation()` is empty, so the file is primarily an encoding/access descriptor for table generation. Privilege tests should confirm that access modes map to the expected `Insn.Priv` value and that compressed or reserved encodings are handled intentionally.
<!-- END_FILE_RESEARCH: sources/test-tools/syzkaller/pkg/ifuzz/riscv64/gen/inst/D/flt.d.yaml -->

# sources/test-tools/syzkaller/pkg/ifuzz/riscv64/gen/inst/D/fmsub.d.yaml

## Purpose

`fmsub.d.yaml` is a riscv-unified-db YAML descriptor for the `fmsub.d` instruction. It is a `instruction` entry in the `D` instruction subset and represents a double-precision floating-point arithmetic. Its long name is "Floating-Point Multiply-Subtract Double-Precision". The description says: The `fmsub.d` instruction multiplies the values in `fs1` and `fs2`, subtracts the value in `fs3`, and writes the final result to `fd`.

## Important APIs, Types, and Functions

The file is data consumed by `pkg/ifuzz/riscv64/gen/gen.go`, not executable code. The generator unmarshals it into `instYAML`, reading `kind`, `name`, `encoding.match`, `encoding.variables`, and `access.u`/`access.vu`. For this descriptor, `definedBy` resolves to `D, Zdinx`, the assembly form is `fd, fs1, fs2, fs3, rm`, and the encoded variable fields are: `fs3` at `31-27`; `fs2` at `24-20`; `fs1` at `19-15`; `rm` at `14-12`; `fd` at `11-7`.

## Control Flow

During generation, `filepath.WalkDir` finds this YAML file, `yaml.Unmarshal` populates the instruction metadata, and `buildInsn` converts the match string into an opcode and mask. `encoding.match` is 32 bits with 9 fixed bits and 23 variable bits. Each declared variable range is passed through `parseLocations`; split locations produce multiple `InsnField` entries with suffixed names so the fuzzer can fill discontiguous bitfields. If the match length is not 32 bits, the current generator skips the file, which is relevant for compressed 16-bit descriptors.

## State and Persistence Behavior

This source has no runtime state or persistence of its own. Its persistent effect is indirect: when accepted by the generator, it contributes one `riscv64.Insn` to the generated Go table, with `Name=fmsub.d`, fixed opcode/mask bits from the match pattern, generated fields from the variable list, `AsUInt32` initialized to the opcode, and `Priv` derived from access mode. The YAML includes semantic text but the current generator persists only encoding, name, fields, and privilege state.

## Dependencies and Integration Points

The descriptor depends on the riscv-unified-db schema (`inst_schema.json`) and is integrated through `gopkg.in/yaml.v3` unmarshalling in `gen.go`. The generated table imports `github.com/google/syzkaller/pkg/ifuzz/riscv64` and registers instructions with syzkaller's RISC-V ifuzz backend. Architectural integration is through `D, Zdinx` extension availability, privilege access `s=always, u=always, vs=always, vu=always. The generator treats this as non-privileged because U/VU access is not `never`.`, and any operation/Sail snippets kept in the source for humans or future tooling.

## Risks and Edge Cases

The main risk is schema drift: fields such as `left_shift`, `not`, richer `definedBy` constraints, and semantic snippets are not modeled by `instYAML`, so they do not affect fuzz generation. A malformed or non-32-bit match pattern silently drops the instruction. Discontiguous immediate fields rely on range ordering from the YAML and simple `hi-lo` parsing, so encodings with single-bit locations or nonstandard location syntax may be skipped unless the generator is extended. Privilege classification is coarse because only U/VU `never` is checked. For this file, match length is 32, fixed bits are 9, variable bits are 23, and privileged classification is False.

## Test Signals

Good signals are generator tests that include this file, assert that `fmsub.d` appears or is intentionally skipped, and compare the generated opcode/mask/field list against the YAML. Decode/encode round trips in `pkg/ifuzz/riscv64` should validate that randomized operands preserve fixed bits. `operation()` is empty, so the file is primarily an encoding/access descriptor for table generation. Privilege tests should confirm that access modes map to the expected `Insn.Priv` value and that compressed or reserved encodings are handled intentionally.
<!-- END_FILE_RESEARCH: sources/test-tools/syzkaller/pkg/ifuzz/riscv64/gen/inst/D/fmsub.d.yaml -->

# sources/test-tools/syzkaller/pkg/ifuzz/riscv64/gen/inst/D/fnmsub.d.yaml

## Purpose

`fnmsub.d.yaml` is a riscv-unified-db YAML descriptor for the `fnmsub.d` instruction. It is a `instruction` entry in the `D` instruction subset and represents a double-precision floating-point arithmetic. Its long name is "Floating-Point Negate-Multiply-Subtract Double-Precision". The description says: The `fnmsub.d` instruction multiplies the values in `fs1` and `fs2`, negates the product, adds the value in `fs3`, and writes the final result to `fd`.

## Important APIs, Types, and Functions

The file is data consumed by `pkg/ifuzz/riscv64/gen/gen.go`, not executable code. The generator unmarshals it into `instYAML`, reading `kind`, `name`, `encoding.match`, `encoding.variables`, and `access.u`/`access.vu`. For this descriptor, `definedBy` resolves to `D, Zdinx`, the assembly form is `fd, fs1, fs2, fs3, rm`, and the encoded variable fields are: `fs3` at `31-27`; `fs2` at `24-20`; `fs1` at `19-15`; `rm` at `14-12`; `fd` at `11-7`.

## Control Flow

During generation, `filepath.WalkDir` finds this YAML file, `yaml.Unmarshal` populates the instruction metadata, and `buildInsn` converts the match string into an opcode and mask. `encoding.match` is 32 bits with 9 fixed bits and 23 variable bits. Each declared variable range is passed through `parseLocations`; split locations produce multiple `InsnField` entries with suffixed names so the fuzzer can fill discontiguous bitfields. If the match length is not 32 bits, the current generator skips the file, which is relevant for compressed 16-bit descriptors.

## State and Persistence Behavior

This source has no runtime state or persistence of its own. Its persistent effect is indirect: when accepted by the generator, it contributes one `riscv64.Insn` to the generated Go table, with `Name=fnmsub.d`, fixed opcode/mask bits from the match pattern, generated fields from the variable list, `AsUInt32` initialized to the opcode, and `Priv` derived from access mode. The YAML includes semantic text but the current generator persists only encoding, name, fields, and privilege state.

## Dependencies and Integration Points

The descriptor depends on the riscv-unified-db schema (`inst_schema.json`) and is integrated through `gopkg.in/yaml.v3` unmarshalling in `gen.go`. The generated table imports `github.com/google/syzkaller/pkg/ifuzz/riscv64` and registers instructions with syzkaller's RISC-V ifuzz backend. Its long name is "Floating-Point Store Double-Precision". The description says: The `fsd` instruction stores a double-precision value from the floating-point registers to memory. It is guaranteed to execute atomically if the effective address is naturally aligned and XLEN≥64. It doesn't modify the bits being transferred; in particular, the payloads of non-canonical NaNs are preserved.

## Purpose

`fsgnj.d.yaml` is a riscv-unified-db YAML descriptor for the `fsgnj.d` instruction. During generation, `filepath.WalkDir` finds this YAML file, `yaml.Unmarshal` populates the instruction metadata, and `buildInsn` converts the match string into an opcode and mask. `encoding.match` is 32 bits with 19 fixed bits and 13 variable bits. Each declared variable range is passed through `parseLocations`; split locations produce multiple `InsnField` entries with suffixed names so the fuzzer can fill discontiguous bitfields. If the match length is not 32 bits, the current generator skips the file, which is relevant for compressed 16-bit descriptors.

## State and Persistence Behavior

This source has no runtime state or persistence of its own. Its persistent effect is indirect: when accepted by the generator, it contributes one `riscv64.Insn` to the generated Go table, with `Name=fcvt.s.l`, fixed opcode/mask bits from the match pattern, generated fields from the variable list, `AsUInt32` initialized to the opcode, and `Priv` derived from access mode. The YAML includes semantic text but the current generator persists only encoding, name, fields, and privilege state.

## Dependencies and Integration Points

The descriptor depends on the riscv-unified-db schema (`inst_schema.json`) and is integrated through `gopkg.in/yaml.v3` unmarshalling in `gen.go`. The generated table imports `github.com/google/syzkaller/pkg/ifuzz/riscv64` and registers instructions with syzkaller's RISC-V ifuzz backend. Architectural integration is through `F` extension availability, privilege access `s=always, u=always, vs=always, vu=always. The generator treats this as non-privileged because U/VU access is not `never`.`, and any operation/Sail snippets kept in the source for humans or future tooling.

## Risks and Edge Cases

The main risk is schema drift: fields such as `left_shift`, `not`, richer `definedBy` constraints, and semantic snippets are not modeled by `instYAML`, so they do not affect fuzz generation. A malformed or non-32-bit match pattern silently drops the instruction. Discontiguous immediate fields rely on range ordering from the YAML and simple `hi-lo` parsing, so encodings with single-bit locations or nonstandard location syntax may be skipped unless the generator is extended. Privilege classification is coarse because only U/VU `never` is checked. For this file, match length is 32, fixed bits are 19, variable bits are 13, and privileged classification is False.

Good signals are generator tests that include this file, assert that `fcvt.s.l` appears or is intentionally skipped, and compare the generated opcode/mask/field list against the YAML. Decode/encode round trips in `pkg/ifuzz/riscv64` should validate that randomized operands preserve fixed bits. `operation()` is empty, so the file is primarily an encoding/access descriptor for table generation. # sources/test-tools/syzkaller/pkg/ifuzz/riscv64/gen/inst/F/feq.s.yaml

## Purpose

`feq.s.yaml` is a riscv-unified-db YAML descriptor for the `feq.s` instruction. It is a `instruction` entry in the `F` instruction subset and represents a single-precision floating-point classification/comparison/sign operation. Its long name is "Floating-Point Equal Single-Precision". The description says: The `feq.s` instruction writes 1 to `xd` if `fs1` and `fs2` are equal, and 0 otherwise. If either operand is NaN, the result is 0 (not equal). If either operand is a signaling NaN, the invalid flag is set. Positive zero is considered equal to negative zero.

## Important APIs, Types, and Functions

The file is data consumed by `pkg/ifuzz/riscv64/gen/gen.go`, not executable code. The generator unmarshals it into `instYAML`, reading `kind`, `name`, `encoding.match`, `encoding.variables`, and `access.u`/`access.vu`. For this descriptor, `definedBy` resolves to `F`, the assembly form is `xd, fs1, fs2`, and the encoded variable fields are: `fs2` at `24-20`; `fs1` at `19-15`; `xd` at `11-7`.

## Control Flow

During generation, `filepath.WalkDir` finds this YAML file, `yaml.Unmarshal` populates the instruction metadata, and `buildInsn` converts the match string into an opcode and mask. `encoding.match` is 32 bits with 17 fixed bits and 15 variable bits. Each declared variable range is passed through `parseLocations`; split locations produce multiple `InsnField` entries with suffixed names so the fuzzer can fill discontiguous bitfields. If the match length is not 32 bits, the current generator skips the file, which is relevant for compressed 16-bit descriptors.

## State and Persistence Behavior

This source has no runtime state or persistence of its own. Its persistent effect is indirect: when accepted by the generator, it contributes one `riscv64.Insn` to the generated Go table, with `Name=feq.s`, fixed opcode/mask bits from the match pattern, generated fields from the variable list, `AsUInt32` initialized to the opcode, and `Priv` derived from access mode. The YAML includes semantic text but the current generator persists only encoding, name, fields, and privilege state.

## Dependencies and Integration Points

The descriptor depends on the riscv-unified-db schema (`inst_schema.json`) and is integrated through `gopkg.in/yaml.v3` unmarshalling in `gen.go`. The generated table imports `github.com/google/syzkaller/pkg/ifuzz/riscv64` and registers instructions with syzkaller's RISC-V ifuzz backend. Architectural integration is through `F` extension availability, privilege access `s=always, u=always, vs=always, vu=always. The generator treats this as non-privileged because U/VU access is not `never`.`, and any operation/Sail snippets kept in the source for humans or future tooling.

## Risks and Edge Cases

The main risk is schema drift: fields such as `left_shift`, `not`, richer `definedBy` constraints, and semantic snippets are not modeled by `instYAML`, so they do not affect fuzz generation. A malformed or non-32-bit match pattern silently drops the instruction. Discontiguous immediate fields rely on range ordering from the YAML and simple `hi-lo` parsing, so encodings with single-bit locations or nonstandard location syntax may be skipped unless the generator is extended. Privilege classification is coarse because only U/VU `never` is checked. For this file, match length is 32, fixed bits are 17, variable bits are 15, and privileged classification is False.

## Test Signals

Good signals are generator tests that include this file, assert that `feq.s` appears or is intentionally skipped, and compare the generated opcode/mask/field list against the YAML. Decode/encode round trips in `pkg/ifuzz/riscv64` should validate that randomized operands preserve fixed bits. `operation()` is present and starts with `check_f_ok($encoding); Bits<32> sp_value_a = f[fs1][31:0]; Bits<32> sp_value_b = f[fs1][31:0]; if (i`. A Sail semantic snippet is also embedded for architecture-model traceability. Privilege tests should confirm that access modes map to the expected `Insn.Priv` value and that compressed or reserved encodings are handled intentionally.
<!-- END_FILE_RESEARCH: sources/test-tools/syzkaller/pkg/ifuzz/riscv64/gen/inst/F/feq.s.yaml -->

## Purpose

`fmax.s.yaml` is a riscv-unified-db YAML descriptor for the `fmax.s` instruction. It is a `instruction` entry in the `F` instruction subset and represents a single-precision floating-point arithmetic. Its long name is "Floating-Point Maximum-Number Single-Precision". The description says: The `fmax.s` instruction writes larger of `fs1` and `fs2` to `fd`. For the purposes of this instruction, the value `-0.0` is considered to be less than the value `+0.0`. If both inputs are NaNs, the result is the canonical NaN. If only one operand is a NaN, the result is the non-NaN operand. Signaling NaN inputs set...

During generation, `filepath.WalkDir` finds this YAML file, `yaml.Unmarshal` populates the instruction metadata, and `buildInsn` converts the match string into an opcode and mask. `encoding.match` is 32 bits with 17 fixed bits and 15 variable bits. If the match length is not 32 bits, the current generator skips the file, which is relevant for compressed 16-bit descriptors.

This source has no runtime state or persistence of its own. Its persistent effect is indirect: when accepted by the generator, it contributes one `riscv64.Insn` to the generated Go table, with `Name=fmax.s`, fixed opcode/mask bits from the match pattern, generated fields from the variable list, `AsUInt32` initialized to the opcode, and `Priv` derived from access mode. For this descriptor, `definedBy` resolves to `F`, the assembly form is `fd, fs1, fs2, fs3, rm`, and the encoded variable fields are: `fs3` at `31-27`; `fs2` at `24-20`; `fs1` at `19-15`; `rm` at `14-12`; `fd` at `11-7`.

## Control Flow

During generation, `filepath.WalkDir` finds this YAML file, `yaml.Unmarshal` populates the instruction metadata, and `buildInsn` converts the match string into an opcode and mask. `encoding.match` is 32 bits with 9 fixed bits and 23 variable bits. ## Purpose

`fsgnj.s.yaml` is a riscv-unified-db YAML descriptor for the `fsgnj.s` instruction. It is a `instruction` entry in the `F` instruction subset and represents a single-precision floating-point classification/comparison/sign operation. Its long name is "Floating-Point Sign-Inject Single-Precision". The description says: The `fsgnj.s` instruction produces a result that takes all bits except the sign bit from `fs1`. The result's sign bit is taken from `fs2`'s sign bit, and the result is written to the destination register `fd`. A Sail semantic snippet is also embedded for architecture-model traceability. Privilege tests should confirm that access modes map to the expected `Insn.Priv` value and that compressed or reserved encodings are handled intentionally.

# sources/test-tools/syzkaller/pkg/ifuzz/riscv64/gen/inst/F/fsw.yaml

## Purpose

`fsw.yaml` is a riscv-unified-db YAML descriptor for the `fsw` instruction. It is a `instruction` entry in the `F` instruction subset and represents a single-precision floating-point memory access. Its long name is "Floating-Point Store Single-Precision". The description says: The `fsw` instruction stores a single-precision floating-point value in `fs2` to memory at address `xs1` + `imm`. It does not modify the bits being transferred; in particular, the payloads of non-canonical NaNs are preserved.

## Important APIs, Types, and Functions

The file is data consumed by `pkg/ifuzz/riscv64/gen/gen.go`, not executable code. The generator unmarshals it into `instYAML`, reading `kind`, `name`, `encoding.match`, `encoding.variables`, and `access.u`/`access.vu`. For this descriptor, `definedBy` resolves to `F`, the assembly form is `fs2, imm(xs1)`, and the encoded variable fields are: `imm` at `31-25|11-7`; `fs2` at `24-20`; `xs1` at `19-15`.

## Control Flow

During generation, `filepath.WalkDir` finds this YAML file, `yaml.Unmarshal` populates the instruction metadata, and `buildInsn` converts the match string into an opcode and mask. `encoding.match` is 32 bits with 10 fixed bits and 22 variable bits. Each declared variable range is passed through `parseLocations`; split locations produce multiple `InsnField` entries with suffixed names so the fuzzer can fill discontiguous bitfields. If the match length is not 32 bits, the current generator skips the file, which is relevant for compressed 16-bit descriptors.

## State and Persistence Behavior

This source has no runtime state or persistence of its own. Its persistent effect is indirect: when accepted by the generator, it contributes one `riscv64.Insn` to the generated Go table, with `Name=fsw`, fixed opcode/mask bits from the match pattern, generated fields from the variable list, `AsUInt32` initialized to the opcode, and `Priv` derived from access mode. The YAML includes semantic text but the current generator persists only encoding, name, fields, and privilege state.

## Dependencies and Integration Points

The descriptor depends on the riscv-unified-db schema (`inst_schema.json`) and is integrated through `gopkg.in/yaml.v3` unmarshalling in `gen.go`. The generated table imports `github.com/google/syzkaller/pkg/ifuzz/riscv64` and registers instructions with syzkaller's RISC-V ifuzz backend. Architectural integration is through `F` extension availability, privilege access `s=always, u=always, vs=always, vu=always. The generator treats this as non-privileged because U/VU access is not `never`.`, and any operation/Sail snippets kept in the source for humans or future tooling.

## Risks and Edge Cases

The main risk is schema drift: fields such as `left_shift`, `not`, richer `definedBy` constraints, and semantic snippets are not modeled by `instYAML`, so they do not affect fuzz generation. A malformed or non-32-bit match pattern silently drops the instruction. Discontiguous immediate fields rely on range ordering from the YAML and simple `hi-lo` parsing, so encodings with single-bit locations or nonstandard location syntax may be skipped unless the generator is extended. Privilege classification is coarse because only U/VU `never` is checked. For this file, match length is 32, fixed bits are 10, variable bits are 22, and privileged classification is False.

## Test Signals

Good signals are generator tests that include this file, assert that `fsw` appears or is intentionally skipped, and compare the generated opcode/mask/field list against the YAML. Decode/encode round trips in `pkg/ifuzz/riscv64` should validate that randomized operands preserve fixed bits. `operation()` is present and starts with `check_f_ok($encoding); XReg virtual_address = X[xs1] + $signed(imm); write_memory<32>(virtual_addres`. A Sail semantic snippet is also embedded for architecture-model traceability. Privilege tests should confirm that access modes map to the expected `Insn.Priv` value and that compressed or reserved encodings are handled intentionally.
<!-- END_FILE_RESEARCH: sources/test-tools/syzkaller/pkg/ifuzz/riscv64/gen/inst/F/fsw.yaml -->

The file is data consumed by `pkg/ifuzz/riscv64/gen/gen.go`, not executable code. The generator unmarshals it into `instYAML`, reading `kind`, `name`, `encoding.match`, `encoding.variables`, and `access.u`/`access.vu`. For this descriptor, `definedBy` resolves to `H`, the assembly form is `xd, xs1`, and the encoded variable fields are: `xs1` at `19-15`; `xd` at `11-7`.

## Control Flow

During generation, `filepath.WalkDir` finds this YAML file, `yaml.Unmarshal` populates the instruction metadata, and `buildInsn` converts the match string into an opcode and mask. `encoding.match` is 32 bits with 22 fixed bits and 10 variable bits. Each declared variable range is passed through `parseLocations`; split locations produce multiple `InsnField` entries with suffixed names so the fuzzer can fill discontiguous bitfields. If the match length is not 32 bits, the current generator skips the file, which is relevant for compressed 16-bit descriptors.

## State and Persistence Behavior

This source has no runtime state or persistence of its own. Its persistent effect is indirect: when accepted by the generator, it contributes one `riscv64.Insn` to the generated Go table, with `Name=hlv.hu`, fixed opcode/mask bits from the match pattern, generated fields from the variable list, `AsUInt32` initialized to the opcode, and `Priv` derived from access mode. The YAML includes semantic text but the current generator persists only encoding, name, fields, and privilege state.

## Dependencies and Integration Points

The descriptor depends on the riscv-unified-db schema (`inst_schema.json`) and is integrated through `gopkg.in/yaml.v3` unmarshalling in `gen.go`. The generated table imports `github.com/google/syzkaller/pkg/ifuzz/riscv64` and registers instructions with syzkaller's RISC-V ifuzz backend. Architectural integration is through `H` extension availability, privilege access `s=always, u=always, vs=always, vu=always. The generator treats this as non-privileged because U/VU access is not `never`.`, and any operation/Sail snippets kept in the source for humans or future tooling.

## Risks and Edge Cases

The main risk is schema drift: fields such as `left_shift`, `not`, richer `definedBy` constraints, and semantic snippets are not modeled by `instYAML`, so they do not affect fuzz generation. A malformed or non-32-bit match pattern silently drops the instruction. Discontiguous immediate fields rely on range ordering from the YAML and simple `hi-lo` parsing, so encodings with single-bit locations or nonstandard location syntax may be skipped unless the generator is extended. Privilege classification is coarse because only U/VU `never` is checked. For this file, match length is 32, fixed bits are 22, variable bits are 10, and privileged classification is False.

## Test Signals

Good signals are generator tests that include this file, assert that `hlv.hu` appears or is intentionally skipped, and compare the generated opcode/mask/field list against the YAML. Decode/encode round trips in `pkg/ifuzz/riscv64` should validate that randomized operands preserve fixed bits. `operation()` is empty, so the file is primarily an encoding/access descriptor for table generation. Privilege tests should confirm that access modes map to the expected `Insn.Priv` value and that compressed or reserved encodings are handled intentionally.

## Purpose

`add.yaml` is a riscv-unified-db YAML descriptor for the `add` instruction. It is a `instruction` entry in the `I` instruction subset and represents a base integer arithmetic/logical operation. Its long name is "Integer add". The description says: Add the value in xs1 to xs2, and store the result in xd. Any overflow is thrown away.

## Important APIs, Types, and Functions

The file is data consumed by `pkg/ifuzz/riscv64/gen/gen.go`, not executable code. The generator unmarshals it into `instYAML`, reading `kind`, `name`, `encoding.match`, `encoding.variables`, and `access.u`/`access.vu`. ## Control Flow

During generation, `filepath.WalkDir` finds this YAML file, `yaml.Unmarshal` populates the instruction metadata, and `buildInsn` converts the match string into an opcode and mask. `encoding.match` is 32 bits with 17 fixed bits and 15 variable bits. Each declared variable range is passed through `parseLocations`; split locations produce multiple `InsnField` entries with suffixed names so the fuzzer can fill discontiguous bitfields. If the match length is not 32 bits, the current generator skips the file, which is relevant for compressed 16-bit descriptors.

## State and Persistence Behavior

This source has no runtime state or persistence of its own. Its persistent effect is indirect: when accepted by the generator, it contributes one `riscv64.Insn` to the generated Go table, with `Name=add`, fixed opcode/mask bits from the match pattern, generated fields from the variable list, `AsUInt32` initialized to the opcode, and `Priv` derived from access mode. The YAML includes semantic text but the current generator persists only encoding, name, fields, and privilege state.

## Dependencies and Integration Points

The descriptor depends on the riscv-unified-db schema (`inst_schema.json`) and is integrated through `gopkg.in/yaml.v3` unmarshalling in `gen.go`. The generated table imports `github.com/google/syzkaller/pkg/ifuzz/riscv64` and registers instructions with syzkaller's RISC-V ifuzz backend. Architectural integration is through `I` extension availability, privilege access `s=always, u=always, vs=always, vu=always. The generator treats this as non-privileged because U/VU access is not `never`.`, and any operation/Sail snippets kept in the source for humans or future tooling.

## Risks and Edge Cases

The main risk is schema drift: fields such as `left_shift`, `not`, richer `definedBy` constraints, and semantic snippets are not modeled by `instYAML`, so they do not affect fuzz generation. A malformed or non-32-bit match pattern silently drops the instruction. Discontiguous immediate fields rely on range ordering from the YAML and simple `hi-lo` parsing, so encodings with single-bit locations or nonstandard location syntax may be skipped unless the generator is extended. Privilege classification is coarse because only U/VU `never` is checked. For this file, match length is 32, fixed bits are 17, variable bits are 15, and privileged classification is False.

## Test Signals

Good signals are generator tests that include this file, assert that `add` appears or is intentionally skipped, and compare the generated opcode/mask/field list against the YAML. Decode/encode round trips in `pkg/ifuzz/riscv64` should validate that randomized operands preserve fixed bits. `operation()` is present and starts with `X[xd] = X[xs1] + X[xs2];`. A Sail semantic snippet is also embedded for architecture-model traceability. # sources/test-tools/syzkaller/pkg/ifuzz/riscv64/gen/inst/I/auipc.yaml

## Purpose

`auipc.yaml` is a riscv-unified-db YAML descriptor for the `auipc` instruction. It is a `instruction` entry in the `I` instruction subset and represents a base integer arithmetic/logical operation. Its long name is "Add upper immediate to pc". The description says: Add an immediate to the current PC.

## Important APIs, Types, and Functions

The file is data consumed by `pkg/ifuzz/riscv64/gen/gen.go`, not executable code. The generator unmarshals it into `instYAML`, reading `kind`, `name`, `encoding.match`, `encoding.variables`, and `access.u`/`access.vu`. For this descriptor, `definedBy` resolves to `I`, the assembly form is `xd, imm`, and the encoded variable fields are: `imm` at `31-12` (left_shift=12); `xd` at `11-7`.

## Control Flow

During generation, `filepath.WalkDir` finds this YAML file, `yaml.Unmarshal` populates the instruction metadata, and `buildInsn` converts the match string into an opcode and mask. `encoding.match` is 32 bits with 7 fixed bits and 25 variable bits. Each declared variable range is passed through `parseLocations`; split locations produce multiple `InsnField` entries with suffixed names so the fuzzer can fill discontiguous bitfields. If the match length is not 32 bits, the current generator skips the file, which is relevant for compressed 16-bit descriptors.

## State and Persistence Behavior

This source has no runtime state or persistence of its own. Its persistent effect is indirect: when accepted by the generator, it contributes one `riscv64.Insn` to the generated Go table, with `Name=auipc`, fixed opcode/mask bits from the match pattern, generated fields from the variable list, `AsUInt32` initialized to the opcode, and `Priv` derived from access mode. The YAML includes semantic text but the current generator persists only encoding, name, fields, and privilege state.

## Dependencies and Integration Points

The descriptor depends on the riscv-unified-db schema (`inst_schema.json`) and is integrated through `gopkg.in/yaml.v3` unmarshalling in `gen.go`. The generated table imports `github.com/google/syzkaller/pkg/ifuzz/riscv64` and registers instructions with syzkaller's RISC-V ifuzz backend. Architectural integration is through `I` extension availability, privilege access `s=always, u=always, vs=always, vu=always. The generator treats this as non-privileged because U/VU access is not `never`.`, and any operation/Sail snippets kept in the source for humans or future tooling.

## Risks and Edge Cases

The main risk is schema drift: fields such as `left_shift`, `not`, richer `definedBy` constraints, and semantic snippets are not modeled by `instYAML`, so they do not affect fuzz generation. A malformed or non-32-bit match pattern silently drops the instruction. Discontiguous immediate fields rely on range ordering from the YAML and simple `hi-lo` parsing, so encodings with single-bit locations or nonstandard location syntax may be skipped unless the generator is extended. Privilege classification is coarse because only U/VU `never` is checked. For this file, match length is 32, fixed bits are 7, variable bits are 25, and privileged classification is False.

## Test Signals

Good signals are generator tests that include this file, assert that `auipc` appears or is intentionally skipped, and compare the generated opcode/mask/field list against the YAML. Decode/encode round trips in `pkg/ifuzz/riscv64` should validate that randomized operands preserve fixed bits. `operation()` is present and starts with `X[xd] = $pc + $signed(imm);`. A Sail semantic snippet is also embedded for architecture-model traceability. Privilege tests should confirm that access modes map to the expected `Insn.Priv` value and that compressed or reserved encodings are handled intentionally.

The descriptor depends on the riscv-unified-db schema (`inst_schema.json`) and is integrated through `gopkg.in/yaml.v3` unmarshalling in `gen.go`. The main risk is schema drift: fields such as `left_shift`, `not`, richer `definedBy` constraints, and semantic snippets are not modeled by `instYAML`, so they do not affect fuzz generation. Privilege classification is coarse because only U/VU `never` is checked. For this file, match length is 32, fixed bits are 10, variable bits are 22, and privileged classification is False.

Good signals are generator tests that include this file, assert that `lwu` appears or is intentionally skipped, and compare the generated opcode/mask/field list against the YAML.
